### [1] [RISE: Adaptive Imagination for World Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20430)
*Hongbo Lu,Liang Yao,Chenghao He,Hao Han,Fan Liu,Wenlong Liao,Tao He,Pai Peng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RISE is an adaptive imagination framework for World Action Models (WAMs) in autonomous driving planning that makes sequential Roll/Stop decisions based on expected planning benefit. It includes a Latent Evaluator for risk estimation and a Rollout Gate for cost-benefit tradeoffs. The system is trained on CounterDrive, a novel counterfactual dataset with diverse outcomes and risk levels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing WAM methods allocate a fixed imagination budget to every scene, which is inefficient since not all scenarios require the same level of planning depth. Some scenes have obvious optimal actions while others require deeper lookahead. The authors identified this inefficiency as the core limitation to address.

Method: RISE uses two key components: (1) A Latent Evaluator that estimates risk revealed by the current planning prefix and potential improvement from continued rollout; (2) A Rollout Gate that decides whether to continue or stop based on expected benefit vs. computation cost. The system is trained on CounterDrive, a counterfactual dataset constructed from factual driving logs to expose diverse outcomes and risk levels, with expert annotations for trajectory validity, incident onset, and causal categories.

Result: RISE achieves the best overall planning performance on NAVSIM and nuScenes datasets while reducing unnecessary rollout computations. Additional transfer experiments demonstrate its plug-in generality across different WAM architectures.

Conclusion: Adaptive imagination allocation significantly improves efficiency and performance in driving planning. The proposed RISE framework successfully balances planning quality with computational cost, and the CounterDrive dataset provides valuable supervision for safety-critical world-modeling research.

Abstract: World Action Models (WAMs) improve planning by incorporating future world evolution into action generation, yet existing methods allocate a fixed imagination budget to every scene. We propose RISE (\textbf{R}efining \textbf{I}magination through \textbf{SE}lective Rollout), a system-level adaptive imagination framework that makes sequential \textsc{Roll}/\textsc{Stop} decisions according to the expected planning benefit of continued rollout. At each step, a Latent Evaluator estimates the risk revealed by the current prefix and how much planning could improve if imagination continues, while a Rollout Gate weighs this expected benefit against additional computation cost. Since factual driving logs expose only one realized future, we further construct \textbf{CounterDrive}, a counterfactual dataset with diverse outcomes and risk levels, to enrich future dynamics and provide localized risk supervision. Each retained sample undergoes expert verification and annotation of trajectory validity, incident onset, and causal category, providing a reusable resource for safety-critical world-modeling research. Experiments on NAVSIM and nuScenes show that RISE achieves the best overall planning performance while reducing unnecessary rollout, with additional transfer results supporting its plug-in generality across WAM architectures.

</details>


### [2] [Recognition-Conditioned Reasoning: A Training-Free Multimodal-LLM Pipeline for Fine-Grained Micro-Action Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21022)
*Fengshun Wang,Jin'ang Han,Zhigang Tu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A training-free, prompt-only system using frozen multimodal LLMs that won first place in the Micro-Action Challenge (MA-Bench) fine-grained understanding track. It dynamically routes tasks to either discriminative or generative MLLMs based on task type, achieving significantly better performance on open-ended tasks (2.68 vs 1.44 average score).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Micro-actions (subtle body movements like fidgeting or head tilts) unconsciously leak emotional and psychological states. Existing models fail to describe which body parts move and reason about why a clip warrants a specific fine-grained category - they only assign labels without explanation.

Method: Built entirely on frozen multimodal large language models (MLLMs) without any fine-tuning or ground-truth supervision. The system dynamically routes each of the eight sub-tasks to the empirically best-suited MLLM: a discriminative MLLM for closed-ended recognition tasks and a generative MLLM for open-ended description and reasoning tasks.

Result: Achieved statistically significant performance advantage on open-ended tasks, attaining an average score of 2.68 (on a five-point scale) compared to 1.44 for the second-best approach. Won first place in the fine-grained understanding track (MA-Bench) of MAC~2026 Micro-Action Challenge.

Conclusion: The dynamic routing approach between discriminative and generative MLLMs based on task type is effective for fine-grained micro-action understanding. Training-free, prompt-only methods can achieve competitive or superior results compared to approaches requiring fine-tuning or ground-truth supervision.

Abstract: Micro-actions are subtle, short, low-amplitude body movements, such as a fidgeting hand or a slight head tilt, that humans perform with little conscious intent yet that reliably leak emotional and psychological state. Understanding them goes beyond assigning a label: a model must also describe which body parts move and reason, faithfully, about why a clip warrants a particular fine-grained category. We present the training-free, prompt-only system that won first place in the fine-grained understanding track (MA-Bench) of the MAC~2026 Micro-Action Challenge, where both fine-tuning and ground-truth supervision are disallowed. Built entirely upon frozen multimodal large language models (MLLMs), the system dynamically routes each of the eight sub-tasks to the MLLM empirically best suited for that task: a discriminative MLLM for closed-ended recognition tasks and a generative MLLM for open-ended description and reasoning tasks. This architecture achieves a statistically significant performance advantage on open-ended tasks, attaining an average score of 2.68 (on a five-point scale) compared to 1.44 for the second-best approach.

</details>


### [3] [Aggregating Visual Information with Optimal Transport for VideoLM Token Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20473)
*Wenti Yin,Xiaotian Han,Junyuan Shang,Yuchen Ding,Shuohuan Wang,Dianhai Yu,Changxin Gao,Nong Sang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AVIOT is a video token compression method that uses optimal transport to aggregate visual information from dense frame observations into compact representations. It preserves question-relevant and spatial information through task-adaptive transport plans and multi-granularity spatial processing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Video language models process videos as dense visual-token sequences with substantial representational redundancy, making compression essential. The central challenge is preserving visual information dispersed across frames under compression.

Method: AVIOT casts video token compression as an optimal transport problem - transporting dense empirical measure of frame observations onto compact target measure. This creates source-to-target coupling that induces distribution over source observations for each target support. Two key adaptations: (1) Question conditioning modulates transport cost and allocates supports to question-relevant content; (2) Multi-granularity spatial processing computes region-specific temporal transport plans and adaptively fuses representations.

Result: AVIOT matches or outperforms the uncompressed baseline on multiple video-understanding benchmarks while retaining strong performance at higher compression ratios.

Conclusion: Optimal transport provides an effective framework for video token compression that can preserve both task-relevant and spatial information, enabling significant compression without performance degradation.

Abstract: Video language models process videos as dense visual-token sequences with substantial representational redundancy. Compressing these sequences is therefore essential for reducing the visual-token burden on language-model decoding. The central challenge is to preserve visual information dispersed across frames under such compression. To this end, we introduce Aggregating Visual Information with Optimal Transport (AVIOT), which casts video token compression as transporting a dense empirical measure of frame observations onto a compact target measure. The resulting source-to-target coupling induces a distribution over source observations for each target support, directly specifying how the compressed video representation is constructed. We further adapt this construction along task and spatial axes. Question conditioning modulates the transport cost between source frames and target supports, while influencing how many supports are allocated to each temporal segment, thereby directing representation capacity toward question-relevant content. At multiple spatial granularities, AVIOT computes region-specific temporal transport plans and adaptively fuses the representations they yield, allowing different regions within the same compact representation to draw from different moments. Evaluations across varying compression ratios show that AVIOT matches or outperforms the uncompressed baseline on multiple video-understanding benchmarks while retaining strong performance at higher compression ratios.

</details>


### [4] [Annotations as Rollouts: Efficient and Scalable Reinforcement Learning for Video MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20492)
*Yunheng Li,Guohong Mu,Hao Li,Shengsheng Qian,Dingwen Zhang,Qibin Hou,Ming-Ming Cheng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: OraRL is a novel RL post-training method for video MLLMs that addresses 'advantage inversion' by using a decoupled advantage estimator with sign-balanced pruning. It achieves 2.2x step time (vs 4.9x for GRPO), works across model scales (0.8B-9B), and outperforms prior models on temporal understanding, tracking, segmentation, and spatial intelligence benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing RL methods for post-training video MLLMs sample on-policy groups with few high-quality rollouts, even with costly chain-of-thought generation. The paper identifies an overlooked role for annotations: oracle rollouts can serve as direct positive optimization targets, but direct integration causes 'advantage inversion' where high-reward oracles raise the baseline and invert positive policy advantages.

Method: OraRL uses a decoupled advantage estimator: policy rollouts determine an oracle-free baseline, while the oracle-policy gap modulates both a directional gain and a separate detached oracle advantage. Sign-balanced pruning retains only the oracle and strongest rollouts of each sign, improving efficiency. The method avoids chain-of-thought generation.

Result: OraRL requires only 2.2x step time of SFT (vs 4.9x for GRPO with CoT). Video-ORA-9B decodes at 130ms vs 4,780ms with CoT. It surpasses backbone from 0.8B to 9B and GRPO up to 100k prompts. Results: temporal mIoU 62.5→66.0, tracking AO 73.0→78.2, segmentation 64.3→70.4, spatial-intelligence macro average 51.0→56.1. On VSI-Bench: 73.1 vs 55.0 (GPT-5) and 55.1 (Gemini-3-Pro).

Conclusion: OraRL successfully addresses advantage inversion through decoupled advantage estimation and sign-balanced pruning, enabling efficient RL post-training for video MLLMs. The method scales with model size and data, achieving state-of-the-art results across multiple benchmarks while significantly reducing computational overhead compared to GRPO with chain-of-thought.

Abstract: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have become a prevailing paradigm for unified video perception. However, post-training on large multi-task datasets remains challenging, as existing reinforcement learning methods sample on-policy groups with few high-quality rollouts even with costly chain-of-thought (CoT) generation. In this paper, we study the sample efficiency and scalability of RL post-training for video MLLMs and introduce OraRL. We identify an overlooked role for annotations: Beyond scoring rollouts, each can enter its on-policy group as an oracle rollout, a direct positive optimization target. Direct oracle integration, however, is nontrivial: a high-reward oracle raises the group baseline and inverts otherwise positive policy advantages, a failure we term advantage inversion. At the core of OraRL is a decoupled advantage estimator: policy rollouts determine an oracle-free baseline, while the oracle-policy gap modulates both a directional gain and a separate detached oracle advantage. Sign-balanced pruning improves efficiency: by retaining only the oracle and the strongest rollouts of each sign, OraRL requires just 2.2x the step time of SFT, less than half the 4.9x required by GRPO with CoT. OraRL scales with model size and data, surpassing its backbone from 0.8B to 9B and GRPO up to 100k prompts. Without chain-of-thought, Video-ORA-9B decodes in 130 ms instead of 4,780 ms. Compared with the respective prior best models, it raises temporal mIoU from 62.5 to 66.0, tracking AO from 73.0 to 78.2, segmentation from 64.3 to 70.4, and the three-benchmark spatial-intelligence macro average from 51.0 to 56.1; on VSI-Bench, it scores 73.1 against 55.0 for GPT-5 and 55.1 for Gemini-3-Pro.

</details>


### [5] [TopoSurfel: Closing the Loop between Gaussian Surfels and Meshes for Surface Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20687)
*Chuanjin Fan,Wenjie Chang,Bohao Liao,Yujia Chen,Wenfei Yang,Tianzhu Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TopoSurfel is a novel framework that bridges 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) and continuous mesh reconstruction through a differentiable iso-surfacing process. It addresses limitations in existing 3DGS-based surface extraction methods, which struggle with artifacts and floaters in textureless or occluded regions due to lack of explicit geometric priors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing 3DGS-based reconstruction methods rely on multi-view geometric consistency or local constraints, but lack explicit structured geometric priors during optimization. This leads to structural ambiguities, artifacts, and floaters, particularly in textureless or occluded regions. The authors sought to close the loop between discrete Gaussian surfels and continuous meshes to improve surface reconstruction quality.

Method: The proposed TopoSurfel framework consists of three key components: (1) A non-trainable differentiable iso-surfacing process that dynamically extracts a continuous proxy mesh from Gaussian surfels; (2) Mesh-guided surfel evolution strategy including normal alignment and geometry-aware density control to suppress floaters and fill surface holes; (3) A spatially aware hybrid re-initialization strategy to address initialization challenges in large-scale environments.

Result: Extensive experiments demonstrate that TopoSurfel achieves competitive geometric reconstruction accuracy while maintaining high-quality mesh-based novel view synthesis.

Conclusion: TopoSurfel successfully bridges the gap between 3D Gaussian Splatting and continuous mesh reconstruction through a differentiable pipeline without requiring auxiliary neural networks or extra per-Gaussian parameters. The method effectively suppresses floaters, fills surface holes, and ensures robust reconstruction across complex large-scale scenes.

Abstract: 3D Gaussian Splatting has achieved remarkable success in novel view synthesis. However, extracting high-fidelity surfaces directly from 3DGS remains challenging due to its discrete and unstructured nature. Existing 3DGS-based reconstruction methods typically rely on multi-view geometric consistency or local constraints. Without an explicit structured geometric prior during optimization, these methods often struggle to resolve structural ambiguities, leading to artifacts and floaters, particularly in textureless or occluded regions. To address this limitation, we propose TopoSurfel, a novel framework that closes the loop between Gaussian surfels and continuous meshes. Unlike recent methods that incorporate mesh extraction into the differentiable pipeline by introducing auxiliary neural networks or extra per-Gaussian parameters, we dynamically extract a continuous proxy mesh via a non-trainable differentiable iso-surfacing process. Leveraging this differentiable connection, we introduce a mesh-guided surfel evolution strategy, including normal alignment and geometry-aware density control, to effectively suppress floaters and fill surface holes. Furthermore, to address the initialization challenges in large-scale environments, we propose a spatially aware hybrid re-initialization strategy that ensures robust reconstruction across complex scenes. Extensive experiments demonstrate that TopoSurfel achieves competitive geometric reconstruction accuracy while maintaining high-quality mesh-based novel view synthesis. The code for our method is available at https://github.com/Fan-Treasure/TopoSurfel.

</details>


### [6] [DiffVC-ONE: Diffusion-based Generative Video Compression with One-Step Video Diffusion Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20515)
*Wenzhuo Ma,Zhenzhong Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DiffVC-ONE is a diffusion-based generative video compression framework that achieves state-of-the-art perceptual quality and temporal consistency at low inference cost by combining a unified latent compressor, a one-step diffusion enhancer, and a hybrid condition generator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing generative video compression methods struggle to simultaneously achieve high temporal consistency and low inference cost while recovering rich visual details at low bitrates.

Method: The framework consists of three key components: (1) Unified Unidirectional Latent Compressor using a shared model to efficiently compress compact latent slices; (2) Video DiT-based One-Step Diffusion Enhancer performing single-step spatio-temporal perceptual enhancement over entire GOPs using reconstructed latents as content anchors; (3) Hybrid Condition Generator extracting structural, strength, and semantic conditions from reconstructed content and quantization information.

Result: Extensive experiments on multiple standard benchmarks demonstrate state-of-the-art perceptual quality and temporal consistency with low inference cost.

Conclusion: DiffVC-ONE successfully addresses the trade-off between perceptual quality, temporal consistency, and inference efficiency in generative video compression through its novel one-step diffusion architecture and hybrid conditioning mechanism.

Abstract: Generative video compression can recover rich visual details at low bitrates, but simultaneously achieving high temporal consistency and low inference cost remains challenging. To address this issue, we propose DiffVC-ONE, a diffusion-based generative video compression framework built on a one-step Video Diffusion Transformer. First, we introduce a Unified Unidirectional Latent Compressor that uses a shared model to efficiently and uniformly compress compact latent slices. We then develop a Video DiT-based One-Step Diffusion Enhancer that uses the reconstructed latent slices as content anchors and performs single-step spatio-temporal perceptual enhancement over an entire group of pictures. Finally, a Hybrid Condition Generator extracts structural, strength, and semantic conditions from the reconstructed content and quantization information. These conditions preserve faithful regions, control the degree of generative enhancement, and supplement content-aware perceptual details during one-step diffusion enhancement. Extensive experiments on multiple standard benchmarks demonstrate that DiffVC-ONE achieves state-of-the-art perceptual quality and temporal consistency with low inference cost.

</details>


### [7] [Grounded-Exo2Ego: Structured Semantic Grounding for Robust Exocentric-to-Egocentric Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20534)
*Shengze Wang,Michael Stengel,Tianye Li,Seonwook Park,Amrita Mazumdar,Koki Nagano,Alex Trevithick,Shalini De Mello*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents Grounded-Exo2Ego, a dual-branch video diffusion model that generates egocentric (first-person) videos from exocentric (third-person) videos for AR/VR and physical AI applications. It addresses extreme view changes and large unobservable regions through geometric anchoring and semantic grounding branches, along with a camera re-localization algorithm and synthetic data engine.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Exo-to-ego video generation is an emerging important topic for AR/VR and physical AI. Unlike conventional novel view synthesis, this task is significantly harder because standard geometric conditioning becomes unreliable under extreme view changes and large unobservable regions. Additionally, camera-reconstruction misalignment was a previously overlooked issue severely undermining exo-to-ego learning.

Method: Grounded-Exo2Ego employs a dual-branch video diffusion architecture: (1) A geometric anchoring branch that conditions generation on rendering of 3D reconstruction, and (2) A semantic grounding branch that goes beyond geometry-based approaches by synthesizing challenging regions based on object-level context. A camera re-localization algorithm is introduced to resolve camera-reconstruction misalignment. A fully automated synthetic data engine generates and renders rigged 3D characters in procedurally generated environments for training.

Result: Evaluation on the challenging EgoExo4D dataset shows that the method outperforms recent state-of-the-art approaches by large margins across all metrics. Detailed ablations validate improvements from each contribution at both data and architectural levels.

Conclusion: The proposed Grounded-Exo2Ego framework successfully addresses the exo-to-ego generation challenge through its dual-branch architecture combining geometric and semantic approaches, along with solutions for camera misalignment and data generation. The method achieves state-of-the-art performance, demonstrating the effectiveness of coupling geometric anchoring with semantic grounding for handling extreme view changes and unobservable regions.

Abstract: Generating egocentric video from a single exocentric video is an emerging and important topic for AR/VR and physical AI. Compared with conventional novel view synthesis, exo-to-ego generation is a significantly harder task because the standard geometric conditioning becomes highly unreliable under extreme view changes and large unobservable regions. We present Grounded-Exo2Ego, a principled framework that addresses these challenges at both the architectural and data levels. Architecturally, Grounded-Exo2Ego is a dual-branch video diffusion model that couples a geometric anchoring branch, which conditions the generation on the rendering of a 3D reconstruction, with a novel semantic grounding branch, which goes beyond the prevailing geometry-based approach and improves quality by synthesizing challenging regions based on object-level context. Additionally, we found that the overlooked issue of camera-reconstruction misalignment severely undermines exo-to-ego learning. We thus introduce a camera re-localization algorithm that resolves this issue and substantially improves quality across all metrics. We further develop a fully automated synthetic data engine that generates and renders rigged 3D characters in procedurally generated environments. Evaluation on the challenging EgoExo4D dataset shows that our method outperforms recent state-of-the-art approaches by large margins across all metrics. Detailed ablations validate improvements from each of our contributions at both the data and architectural level.

</details>


### [8] [Keep Your Friends Close, and the Right Neighbours Closer: Disaster-Conditioned Kernel-Regularized Graph Attention for Building Damage Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20548)
*Fuad Hasan,Chul Min Yeum*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper addresses spatial context utilization in disaster damage classification, proposing a disaster-type-conditioned graph model with learnable multi-scale spatial kernels and a residual de-correlation loss to avoid naive smoothing. The method improves macro-F1 and reduces spatial autocorrelation errors under event shift on xBD and cross-dataset transfer to Ida-BD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Disaster damage is inherently spatial—buildings rarely fail in isolation. However, existing pipelines rely primarily on per-building appearance cues despite spatially structured uncertainty. The challenge is that different disaster types (floods, hurricanes, wildfires) exhibit different clustering behaviors, making spatial reasoning valuable but prone to misuse through naive context aggregation that oversmooths boundaries or propagates structured errors.

Method: The approach uses a post-localization, classification-only setup on the xBD dataset. Buildings are represented by pre/post combined (PPC) patches cropped from polygons. Spatial context is modeled with GPS-derived building graphs. A disaster-type-conditioned graph model injects a learnable multi-scale spatial kernel prior into attention, allowing the effective neighborhood scale to adapt across disaster types. A residual de-correlation loss penalizes positive Moran's I in prediction residuals to discourage coherence-by-smoothing. Evaluation uses leave-one-event-out (LOEO) protocol on xBD and cross-dataset transfer from xBD to Ida-BD.

Result: The model improves macro-F1 and substantially reduces residual spatial autocorrelation under zero-shot event shift, indicating better use of spatial context rather than naive smoothing. This enables more reliable transfer to unseen events within known disaster types.

Conclusion: The proposed method successfully addresses the tension between utilizing spatial context and avoiding oversmoothing. By conditioning spatial reasoning on disaster type and penalizing spatial autocorrelation in residuals, the approach achieves better generalization to unseen events. This work demonstrates that spatial context can be effectively leveraged for disaster damage classification when properly structured, paving the way for more reliable damage assessment in real-world disaster response scenarios.

Abstract: Disaster damage is spatial: buildings rarely fail in isolation. Yet using spatial context for damage classification remains surprisingly underexplored, and many pipelines still rely primarily on per-building appearance cues even when the dominant uncertainty is spatially structured. Complicating matters, the right neighbourhood is not the same across events. Floods, hurricanes, and wildfires can exhibit very different clustering behaviour, making spatial reasoning valuable but easy to misuse - naive context aggregation can improve visual coherence while oversmoothing boundaries or propagating structured errors. We study this tension on xBD (the dataset used in the xView2 challenge) in a controlled post-localization, classification-only setup: each building is represented by a pre/post combined (PPC) patch cropped from the provided polygons, and spatial context is modelled with GPS-derived building graphs. Our approach keeps local evidence "close" by preserving strong spatial relationships in disaster damage patterns, while bringing only the right neighbours "closer" through a disaster-type-conditioned graph model that injects a learnable multi-scale spatial kernel prior into attention, allowing the effective neighbourhood scale to adapt across disaster types rather than being learned as a single global smoothing rule. To discourage coherence-by-smoothing, we add a residual de-correlation loss that penalizes positive Moran's~I in prediction residuals. We evaluate the method under event and dataset shift with a leave-one-event-out (LOEO) protocol on xBD and cross-dataset transfer from xBD to Ida-BD. The model improves macro-F1 and substantially reduces residual spatial autocorrelation under zero-shot event shift, indicating better use of spatial context rather than naive smoothing and enabling more reliable transfer to unseen events within known disaster types.

</details>


### [9] [Learning Prostate Anatomy at Test Time for Cancer Detection in Micro-Ultrasound](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20557)
*Obed Korshie Dzikunu,Mohammad Mahdi Abootorabi,Mohamed Harmanani,Paul F. R. Wilson,Emma Willis,Ferdinand Luger,Adam Kinnaird,Brian Wodlinger,Parvin Mousavi,Purang Abolmaesumi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ANT is a test-time adaptation framework that addresses domain shift in prostate cancer detection by using an auxiliary prostate segmentation task supervised by pseudo-masks from a frozen pretrained network. This approach aligns encoder representations to target domain anatomy while preserving cancer-discriminative features.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Domain shift across clinical centers using different imaging hardware or acquisition protocols is a fundamental barrier to deploying deep learning models for prostate cancer detection. Existing TTA methods only correct for statistical differences in image appearance but ignore the anatomical structure of the target domain.

Method: ANT adapts a pretrained cancer detection encoder to the target domain by solving an auxiliary prostate segmentation task at test time, supervised by pseudo-masks from a frozen pretrained segmentation network. By aligning encoder representations to prostate anatomy in the target domain, it corrects domain-specific feature drift while preserving cancer-discriminative structure.

Result: Under a leave-one-center-out protocol, ANT improves mean AUC by 2.9% at the biopsy-core level and 3.6% at the patient level over no adaptation, outperforming TTA baselines. Training: 693 patients (earlier-generation micro-ultrasound). Evaluation: 118 patients (newer-generation system across two centers).

Conclusion: Segmentation-guided TTA that leverages anatomical structure is effective for domain adaptation in prostate cancer detection, achieving significant AUC improvements over existing test-time adaptation methods.

Abstract: Domain shift across clinical centers using different imaging hardware or acquisition protocols remains a fundamental barrier to deploying deep learning models for prostate cancer (PCa) detection. Existing test-time adaptation (TTA) methods address distribution shift through entropy minimization or augmentation-based self-supervision, correcting for statistical differences in image appearance but ignoring the anatomical structure of the target domain. We propose ANT, a segmentation-guided TTA framework that adapts a pretrained cancer detection encoder to the target domain by solving an auxiliary prostate segmentation task at test time, supervised by pseudo-masks from a frozen pretrained segmentation network. By aligning encoder representations to prostate anatomy in the target domain, ANT corrects domain-specific feature drift while preserving cancer-discriminative structure. The model was trained on 693 patients imaged with an earlier-generation micro-ultrasound scanner in a multi-center clinical trial, and evaluated on 118 patients acquired with a newer-generation system across two centers in another clinical trial. Under a leave-one-center-out protocol with identical evaluation conditions across all methods, ANT improves mean AUC by 2.9% and 3.6% at the biopsy-core and patient levels, respectively, over no adaptation, outperforming TTA baselines. Code is available at: https://github.com/ObedDzik/ant.git.

</details>


### [10] [Zero-Shot Color Image Manipulation Localization via Noise Residual Artifact Pattern Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20558)
*Edgar Gonzalez-Fernandez*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a zero-shot, training-free blind image manipulation localization pipeline that estimates device-specific artifact patterns from a single suspect image's noise residual without prior training data or device enrollment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing passive forensic methods rely predominantly on the green channel of the Bayer residual, discarding correlated information from other color channels and typically requiring training data or device enrollment. The goal is to develop a training-free approach that can work on a single image.

Method: The pipeline includes: (1) a principled denoiser selection criterion based on acquired-to-interpolated noise variance ratio, (2) block-level correlation analysis against an estimated reference pattern, and (3) a two-component Gaussian Mixture Model scoring stage for pixel-level tampering probability maps. No fixed filter configuration, color layout, or block period is assumed.

Result: The ablation study evaluates denoiser choice and block size impact on localization accuracy. Comparisons against state-of-the-art passive methods demonstrate the competitiveness of the proposed zero-shot approach.

Conclusion: The proposed zero-shot approach achieves competitive performance compared to state-of-the-art passive methods without requiring training data or device enrollment, making it a promising solution for blind image manipulation localization.

Abstract: Digital cameras embed device-specific artifacts into every acquired image through demosaicing, in-camera post-processing, and lossy compression. These traces constitute a forensic signal that can be exploited to assess image authenticity. Existing passive methods rely predominantly on the green channel of the Bayer residual, discarding the correlated information available in the remaining color channels and typically requiring training data or device enrollment. This work proposes a zero-shot, training-free blind image manipulation localization pipeline that estimates a reference artifact pattern directly from the noise residual of a single suspect image, without assuming a fixed filter configuration, color layout, or block period. The pipeline incorporates a principled denoiser selection criterion based on the acquired-to-interpolated noise variance ratio, a block-level correlation analysis against the estimated reference pattern, and a two-component Gaussian Mixture Model scoring stage that produces a pixel-level tampering probability map. An ablation study evaluates the impact of denoiser choice and block size on localization accuracy, and comparisons against state-of-the-art passive methods demonstrate the competitiveness of the proposed zero-shot approach.

</details>


### [11] [Aggregate, Don't Adapt: Subject-Level Posterior Aggregation and Transductive Calibration for Cross-Site Parkinsonian Gait Severity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20587)
*Junlong Shen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper describes the winning solution (0.6945 macro-F1) for the MoCha 2026 Benchmark on Parkinsonian Gait prediction, which predicts MDS-UPDRS gait severity from SMPL motion data at unseen clinical sites. The winning approach used a simple 4×512 linear layer on a frozen public motion encoder, with gains primarily from three "bookkeeping" techniques: exact reference benchmark replication, subject-level walk averaging, and transductive calibration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The challenge addresses predicting Parkinson's disease severity (MDS-UPDRS gait scores) from motion capture data at completely unseen clinical sites (domain generalization/transfer learning problem). The key difficulty is generalizing to new sites with different patient populations and recording conditions.

Method: Method: Simple linear classifier (4×512 linear layer) on a frozen public motion encoder. Three key techniques: (1) Exact head recipe replication from reference benchmark, (2) Per-walk posterior averaging within subject groupings, (3) Label-free transductive calibration of feature mean and decision threshold. They tested 4 different encoder fine-tuning approaches (all failed) and 10 alternative encoders (all worse).

Result: Achieved 0.6945 macro-F1 on hidden test, ranking 1st out of 58 entries. Significantly outperformed runner-up (0.5807) and baseline (0.4289). Cross-validation was anti-correlated with test performance, indicating distribution shift between validation and test sets.

Conclusion: The winning margin came almost entirely from three stages typically treated as trivial bookkeeping: exact recipe replication, subject-level aggregation, and transductive calibration. Subject-level aggregation was identified as the binding performance ceiling. This challenges conventional wisdom about the importance of complex model architectures versus careful post-processing and calibration.

Abstract: We describe the winning entry to the MoCha 2026 Benchmark and Challenge on Parkinsonian Gait, which predicts MDS-UPDRS gait severity from canonicalized SMPL motion recorded at clinical sites unseen during training. The system reaches 0.6945 macro-F1 on the hidden test and ranked first of 58 entries, ahead of the runner-up at 0.5807 and the organizers' baseline at 0.4289, on a frozen public motion encoder with a single $4\times512$ linear layer. Nearly all of the margin comes from three stages usually treated as bookkeeping: reproducing the reference benchmark's exact head recipe, averaging per-walk posteriors within the subject grouping the organizers ship, and a label-free transductive calibration of the feature mean and the decision operating point. Fine-tuning the encoder lost in four distinct forms, and ten alternative encoders were worse. Every ablation number is a paid read on the hidden test, because our own leave-two-cohort-out cross-validation proved anti-correlated with the deciding score over eleven configurations. We give the negative record in full, and identify our largest gain, subject-level aggregation, as the binding ceiling on this benchmark.

</details>


### [12] [A Dataset-Centric Benchmark of Deep Learning Methods for Grape Leaf Disease Classification and Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20608)
*Petar Canoski,Vlatko Spasev,Ivica Dimitrovski,Ivan Kitanovski,Petre Lameski*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents a dataset-centric benchmark for grape leaf disease classification and detection using deep learning. It analyzes publicly available datasets, evaluates representative models across classification and detection tasks, and conducts cross-dataset experiments to assess transfer learning capabilities. The study highlights the gap between controlled laboratory conditions and real-world vineyard challenges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation stems from the fact that many deep learning studies on grape leaf disease rely on limited datasets acquired under controlled conditions, which may not represent real-world challenges like complex backgrounds, variable illumination, occlusion, and device differences. There was a need for a comprehensive benchmark that evaluates methods across diverse datasets and examines cross-dataset generalization.

Method: The researchers analyzed publicly available grape leaf disease datasets based on multiple criteria: disease categories, annotation types, acquisition conditions, image characteristics, class distributions, provenance, and task suitability. They evaluated representative models in three settings: (1) image-level classification, (2) region-level classification, and (3) object detection. Classification was assessed using accuracy, while detection used mAP@50 and mAP@50:95. Cross-dataset experiments were conducted to examine transfer between datasets with compatible disease categories but different visual characteristics.

Result: The results show near-saturated classification performance on several controlled or derivative datasets, but greater difficulty on heterogeneous datasets. Detection performance showed substantial variation across annotation settings. Cross-dataset performance drops sharply, especially for object detection, indicating that shared disease labels do not necessarily define equivalent recognition tasks.

Conclusion: The benchmark emphasizes the importance of dataset provenance, realistic field evaluation, annotation compatibility, and external validation for reliable vineyard disease recognition. The study reveals that models performing well on controlled datasets may fail in real-world scenarios, highlighting the need for more diverse and field-acquired datasets for training and evaluation.

Abstract: Grape leaf disease recognition is important for precision agriculture, enabling early diagnosis, timely intervention, and improved vineyard management. Although deep learning has achieved strong results, many studies rely on few datasets, often acquired under controlled conditions, and may not reflect real vineyard challenges such as complex backgrounds, variable illumination, occlusion, leaf pose, disease severity, and device differences. This paper presents a dataset-centric benchmark of deep learning methods for grape leaf disease classification and detection. We analyze publicly available datasets in terms of disease categories, annotation types, acquisition conditions, image characteristics, class distributions, provenance, and task suitability. Representative models are evaluated in three settings: image-level classification, region-level classification, and object detection. Classification is assessed using accuracy, while detection is evaluated using mAP@50 and mAP@50:95. Cross-dataset experiments further examine transfer between datasets with compatible disease categories but different visual and annotation characteristics. Results show near-saturated classification performance on several controlled or derivative datasets, greater difficulty on heterogeneous datasets, and substantial variation in detection performance across annotation settings. Cross-dataset performance drops sharply, especially for object detection, indicating that shared disease labels do not necessarily define equivalent recognition tasks. The benchmark emphasizes dataset provenance, realistic field evaluation, annotation compatibility, and external validation for reliable vineyard disease recognition.

</details>


### [13] [RECOUNT: Reference-guided Counting with Synthetic Visual Exemplars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20621)
*Adriano D'Alessandro,Ali Mahdavi-Amiri,Ghassan Hamarneh*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RECOUNT is a plug-and-play framework for image-guided zero-shot object counting that uses a single off-scene reference image combined with a diffusion model-generated diverse exemplar gallery to improve categorization accuracy while preserving the class-agnostic proposals of any frozen counter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Text-guided zero-shot object counters excel at spatial localization but fail on novel or fine-grained classes because natural language is too coarse to fully specify visual identity, causing failure to separate visually similar distractors. Few-shot counters require manual annotations on every image, making them impractical.

Method: RECOUNT uses a single off-scene reference image to specify the target category visually. A diffusion model is repurposed as an automated contrastive data engine that expands the reference into a diverse exemplar gallery. The framework preserves class-agnostic proposals from any frozen counter and uses a separate visual module (frozen backbone with lightweight head trained on synthetic data) to match proposals against target and distractor galleries.

Result: RECOUNT achieves the best zero-shot accuracy on both benchmarks, cutting counting error (MAE) by 55% on LookAlikes and 21% on PairTally relative to the strongest prior zero-shot counter.

Conclusion: RECOUNT successfully resolves the dilemma between text-guided zero-shot counters (coarse categorization) and few-shot counters (manual annotation requirements) by using visual exemplars from a single reference image augmented by a diffusion model-generated diverse gallery, achieving state-of-the-art zero-shot counting performance.

Abstract: Text-guided zero-shot object counters excel at spatial localization but categorize poorly on novel or fine-grained classes: natural language is too coarse to fully specify visual identity, so they fail to separate visually similar distractors. Few-shot counters sidestep this with visual exemplars, but require manual annotations on every image. To resolve this dilemma, we introduce RECOUNT, a plug-and-play framework for image-guided zero-shot counting. Rather than specify a category with a text prompt, our key insight is to specify it visually, from a single off-scene reference image. However, we find that a lone reference image provides narrow coverage of a category's appearance and is unreliable across diverse scenes. We therefore repurpose a diffusion model as an automated contrastive data engine that expands the reference into a diverse exemplar gallery, supplying the discriminative detail that text cannot. RECOUNT preserves the class-agnostic proposals of any frozen counter and offloads categorization to a separate visual module (a frozen backbone with a lightweight head trained on this synthetic data) that matches each proposal against the target and distractor galleries. Applied to a frozen counter, RECOUNT attains the best zero-shot accuracy on both benchmarks, cutting counting error (MAE) by 55% on LookAlikes and 21% on PairTally relative to the strongest prior zero-shot counter.

</details>


### [14] [MV2GF: Multi-view Pedestrian Detection with a Visual Geometric Foundation Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20639)
*Taiga Yamane,Satoshi Suzuki,Ryo Masumura,Shota Orihashi,Tomohiro Tanaka,Mana Ihori,Naoki Makishima*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes MV2GF, a Multi-View Pedestrian Detection (MVPD) method that leverages a visual geometric foundation model to improve generalization to unseen camera configurations. The approach fuses task-specific features with general-purpose geometric features extracted by the foundation model and uses 3D pointmaps to project pixels to appropriate 3D locations, preventing detection models from depending on training distortion patterns.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current MVPD methods adopt a unified framework that projects 2D image features into 3D world space. However, they struggle to generalize to unseen camera configurations during training due to two main issues: (1) difficulty capturing accurate visual geometry across views in unseen camera configurations, and (2) detection models becoming highly dependent on distortion patterns arising from image feature projection during training.

Method: The proposed MV2GF leverages a visual geometric foundation model that has exhibited strong generalization in capturing visual geometry across views and predicting accurate 3D attributes in diverse camera configurations. The method has two key components: (1) fusing task-specific features with general-purpose geometric features extracted by the foundation model to effectively capture visual geometry even in unseen camera configurations, and (2) projecting each pixel in the image features to appropriate 3D locations using 3D pointmaps predicted by the foundation model, preventing the detection model from depending on distortion patterns during training.

Result: Experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of leveraging a visual geometric foundation model for MVPD and show that MV2GF generalizes better than existing methods to unseen camera configurations.

Conclusion: The visual geometric foundation model approach is effective for MVPD tasks. MV2GF achieves superior generalization performance compared to existing methods, successfully addressing the challenges of generalizing to unseen camera configurations by using geometric foundation features and 3D pointmap-based projection.

Abstract: Multi-View Pedestrian Detection (MVPD) aims to detect pedestrians in the form of a bird's eye view map from multi-view images. Recent MVPD methods adopt a unified framework that projects 2D image features into a 3D world space and aggregates them into a single feature. Although they are effective, they struggle to generalize to unseen camera configurations during training due to two main issues. First, they are difficult to capture accurate visual geometry across views in unseen camera configurations. Second, they make detection models highly dependent on distortion patterns during training arising from their image feature projection. To address these, we leverage a visual geometric foundation model and propose MV2GF. This foundation model has exhibited strong generalization in capturing visual geometry across views and predicting accurate 3D attributes in diverse camera configurations. MV2GF fuses task-specific features with general-purpose geometric features extracted by the foundation model to effectively capture the visual geometry even in unseen camera configurations. Furthermore, MV2GF projects each pixel in the image features to an appropriate 3D location using 3D pointmaps predicted by the foundation model, preventing the detection model from depending on distortion patterns during training. Our experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of leveraging a visual geometric foundation model for MVPD and that MV2GF generalizes better than existing methods.

</details>


### [15] [Lift, Associate, and Fuse: A Decision-Centric Framework for 2D-to-3D Foundation Model Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20659)
*Wentao Sun,Yiping Chen,John S. Zelek,Jonathan Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LAF (Lift, Associate, and Fuse) is a decision-centric framework that decomposes 3D segmentation systems transfering 2D foundation model predictions into five operators (Generate, Associate, Reconcile, Fuse, Persist/Query). Applied to 161 systems, it exposes four recurring properties about identity, carrier design, evaluation, and qualifier terminology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current grouping of 2D-to-3D transfer methods by task or representation obscures critical design decisions about evidence grounding, identity establishment, conflict handling, information fusion, and state persistence. This lack of systematic analysis prevents meaningful comparison and diagnosis of failures across systems.

Method: LAF defines five operators with explicit contracts for the persistent carrier (spatial support, semantic state, identity state, uncertainty, provenance, operations). The framework was operationalized as a structured audit protocol and applied to 161 systems spanning various carrier types (point, field, Gaussian, object, graph, memory-based). Four stress tests (representation, temporal, relational, feed-forward) were conducted.

Result: Analysis revealed four recurring properties: (1) Association does not establish identity; (2) Carrier design fixes both query interface and correction boundary; (3) Rendered-view, native-3D, and proposal-level evaluations are not interchangeable; (4) Qualifiers like "training-free," "real-time," "open-vocabulary," "generalizable" require stage and cost ledger attachment for meaning.

Conclusion: LAF provides a representation-neutral method for comparing existing systems, diagnosing irreversible failures, and specifying revisable 3D perception for future agents. The framework enables systematic analysis of design decisions across diverse 3D segmentation systems.

Abstract: Methods that transfer predictions from two-dimensional foundation models into three-dimensional segmentation are commonly grouped by task or representation. Those groupings obscure the decisions that determine whether a system remains coherent across views: where image evidence is grounded, when observations become one identity, how semantic and granularity conflicts are handled, which information is fused, and what state survives for later queries. We introduce \textbf{Lift, Associate, and Fuse (LAF)}, a decision-centric framework that represents a transfer system as five operators: \textbf{Generate, Associate, Reconcile, Fuse, and Persist/Query}. LAF defines an explicit contract for the persistent carrier---its spatial support, semantic state, identity state, uncertainty, provenance, and supported operations---and identifies the first stage at which discarded evidence becomes unrecoverable. We operationalize the framework as a structured audit protocol and apply it to 161 systems available through 7 August 2026, spanning point-, field-, Gaussian-, object-, graph-, and memory-based carriers. Representation, temporal, relational, and feed-forward stress tests required no additional analytical stage after the final confirmation pass. The resulting decision traces expose four recurring properties: association does not establish identity; carrier design fixes both the query interface and correction boundary; rendered-view, native-3D, and proposal-level evaluations are not interchangeable; and qualifiers such as \emph{training-free}, \emph{real-time}, \emph{open-vocabulary}, and \emph{generalizable} are meaningful only when attached to a stage and a complete cost ledger. LAF therefore supplies a representation-neutral method for comparing existing systems, diagnosing irreversible failures, and specifying revisable 3D perception for future agents.

</details>


### [16] [Shortcut Learning in a Public Grape Disease Dataset: Annotation Granularity as a Modulator, Not a Cause](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20663)
*Pushuo Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper reveals that annotation granularity inconsistency in agricultural disease datasets creates a shortcut vulnerability. One class annotated at whole-leaf level while five others at lesion level causes 65.7% of cross-species false positives, despite good in-distribution metrics. Granularity modulates but doesn't cause this shortcut.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Public agricultural disease datasets are evaluated fit for use based on reported metrics (like mAP), which don't reveal whether the annotation scheme is internally consistent. This inconsistency can create shortcuts that models exploit but remain invisible to standard evaluation.

Method: Experiments on a grape disease dataset (3288 images, 11995 boxes, 6 classes): (1) Testing 5 architectures with varying capacity and input resolution, (2) Analyzing cross-species false positives on 5156 images containing no grape, (3) Counterfactual retraining by shrinking one class's boxes, (4) Placebo control experiment, (5) Opposite-direction manipulation coarsening finest class to whole-leaf level, (6) Developing a granularity screening statistic.

Result: All five architectures show mAP50 range comparable to seed-to-seed noise with bottleneck at small objects. One class (whole-leaf annotation, median box 43.16% of image) accounts for 65.7% of cross-species false positives (13.41x over-represented). Shrinking that class's boxes reduces its false positives by 66%. Coarsening the finest class to whole-leaf level (0.57% to 40.37%) yields zero cross-species false positives while unmanipulated original class holds 50.0% of them.

Conclusion: Annotation granularity modulates but doesn't cause the shortcut: it can amplify or attenuate an existing sink but cannot create one. The failure mode is invisible to in-distribution evaluation. A granularity screening statistic requiring neither images nor training is provided. Airborne lesion-level detection is shown to be optically out of reach.

Abstract: Public datasets for agricultural disease detection are usually judged fit for use from reported metrics, which say nothing about whether the annotation scheme is internally consistent. On one public grape disease dataset (3288 images, 11995 boxes, 6 classes), varying model capacity, input resolution and detection paradigm yields a test-set mAP50 range comparable to seed-to-seed noise, with the bottleneck at small objects across all five architectures. The finding lies on the data side: one class is annotated at whole-leaf level (median box area 43.16% of the image) while the other five are annotated at lesion level. On 5156 cross-species images containing no grape, 65.7% of the false-positive boxes fall into that one class, an over-representation of 13.41x relative to its share of the training annotations. Counterfactual retraining establishes a causal effect of granularity on the magnitude of the shortcut: shrinking only that class's boxes cuts its cross-species false positives by 66%, and a placebo control confirms the effect is specific to the manipulated class. A manipulation in the opposite direction, with criteria registered in advance, returns a negative result: coarsening the finest class to whole-leaf level (0.57% to 40.37%), matched in box count and share of annotations and with higher in-distribution AP, still leaves its cross-species false positives at zero boxes, while the unmanipulated original class holds 50.0% of them. Annotation granularity is therefore a modulator of this shortcut, not its cause: it can amplify or attenuate a sink that already exists, but cannot create one, and what fixes the destination remains open. We also give a granularity screening statistic requiring neither images nor training, and show airborne lesion-level detection to be optically out of reach. The failure mode is invisible to in-distribution evaluation.

</details>


### [17] [Aristotelian Manifolds: Leveraging Platonic Perceptual Features for Backpropagation Free Rapid Concept Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20682)
*Michael Karnes,Alper Yilmaz*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: This paper formalizes and systematically characterizes Aristotelian Manifolds, a generalized structural framework built upon the Platonic Representation Hypothesis. We position high-capacity foundation models as universal perceptual filters and conduct a comprehensive layer-wise investigation to map how knowledge is functionally synthesized within these latent subspaces. Across diverse architectural paradigms and multi-domain datasets, we rigorously chart the interplay between network depth, dimensionality reduction, and distance metrics. Our characterization reveals that semantic maturation does not follow a singular, monotonic path; instead, different data domains exhibit highly distinct geometric response profiles, characterized by intermediate mound-like peaks for specialized clinical modalities and sigmoidal plateaus for natural visual tasks. By profiling the exact coordinates where these manifolds achieve peak representational efficiency, we establish a predictable taxonomy for layer selection and feature compression. Ultimately, this systematic characterization demonstrates that mapping the internal geometry of frozen representations provides a robust, backpropagation-free, and interpretable framework for understanding and exploiting foundation model latent spaces.

</details>


### [18] [Identity-Aware Human-Object Interaction Motion Captioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20690)
*Yiming Wang,Yonghao Dang,Huilai Li,Jiawei Tu,Jianqin Yin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces Identity-Aware Human-Object Interaction Motion Captioning, where generated captions must specify both subject identity and HOI motion (e.g., "Sub_ID lifts the chair") rather than using generic terms like "a person". The proposed ID-HOINet uses multi-view videos for training but supports single-view inference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing HOI motion captioning methods describe actions using generic subject references (e.g., "a person" or "someone") without grounding the caption in specific subject identity, limiting their practical applicability.

Method: ID-HOINet contains two core components: (1) Multi-View Identity-Motion Learning Module (MVIML) - learns from multi-view videos by modeling dependencies across temporal stages and camera viewpoints to capture identity and interaction motion features; (2) Two-Stage Caption Rewriting Strategy (TSCR) - at inference, first retrieves subject identity and generates identity-agnostic HOI motion captions, then rewrites these captions with predicted identity to produce final identity-aware captions.

Result: ID-HOINet achieves state-of-the-art performance on the Identity-Aware HOI motion captioning task, based on experiments conducted on BEHAVE and InterCap datasets.

Conclusion: The proposed ID-HOINet successfully addresses the limitation of generic subject references in HOI motion captioning by introducing identity-aware generation. The multi-view training approach combined with single-view inference capability provides a practical solution for real-world applications.

Abstract: Existing human-object interaction (HOI) motion captioning methods typically describe what happens while referring to the subject using generic terms such as "a person" or "someone", without grounding the caption in subject identity. To address this limitation, we introduce Identity-Aware Human-Object Interaction Motion Captioning task. This task requires each generated caption to specify both the subject identity and the corresponding HOI motion. For example, the model generates "Sub_ID lifts the chair" rather than "A person lifts the chair". For this task, we design identity-aware HOI motion captions based on the BEHAVE and InterCap datasets. We further propose ID-HOINet, which learns from multi-view videos while supporting single-view identity-aware HOI motion caption generation. ID-HOINet contains two core components: Multi-View Identity-Motion Learning Module (MVIML) and Two-Stage Caption Rewriting Strategy (TSCR). MVIML learns from multi-view videos by modeling dependencies across temporal stages and camera viewpoints, capturing identity and interaction motion features. At inference, the TSCR first retrieves the subject identity and generates identity-agnostic HOI motion captions. TSCR then rewrites these captions with the predicted identity to produce the final identity-aware HOI motion captions. Experiments demonstrate that ID-HOINet achieves state-of-the-art performance. Code will be released upon acceptance.

</details>


### [19] [Bridging Language and Spherical Space: Object-Centric Control for Text-to-Panorama Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20691)
*Derui Li,Qian Qiao,Yuhao Sun,Wenhao Guo,Peng Lu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PanoCtrl is an object-centric framework for controllable text-to-panorama generation that addresses the misalignment between directional language and spherical panoramic space. It uses PanoParse to convert text descriptions into structured object-level spherical conditions (semantics + BFoV parameters) and PanoControl to inject this guidance into diffusion transformers via object-aware attention and spatial residual enhancement. A new dataset PanoGround supports training.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Panoramic images are crucial for immersive applications (VR/AR/3D content), but existing text-to-panorama methods rely on implicit spatial reasoning and fail to ground object-level directional descriptions in spherical scenes. Manual layout specification reduces flexibility and doesn't resolve the misalignment between egocentric directional language and panoramic image space.

Method: The framework consists of three components: (1) PanoParse - a text-conditioned parser that predicts object semantics and spherical bounding field-of-view (BFoV) parameters from textual descriptions; (2) PanoControl - a module that injects object-level semantic and spatial guidance into the diffusion transformer through object-aware attention and spatial residual enhancement; (3) PanoGround - a dataset with object-level spherical annotations and diverse directional descriptions for training.

Result: Extensive experiments demonstrate that PanoCtrl achieves state-of-the-art performance in both spatial alignment and image quality for controllable panoramic generation.

Conclusion: The proposed PanoCtrl framework successfully bridges natural language and spherical panoramic space by explicitly converting textual descriptions into structured object-level spherical conditions, providing a more flexible and accurate approach to text-to-panorama generation compared to existing methods.

Abstract: Panoramic image generation is increasingly important for immersive applications such as virtual reality, augmented reality, and 3D content creation. Unlike perspective images, panoramic images represent a viewer-centered $360^\circ$ surrounding space, where directional expressions such as left, right, front, and behind play a central role in spatial understanding. However, existing text-to-panorama methods largely rely on implicit spatial reasoning and often fail to faithfully ground object-level directional descriptions in spherical panoramic scenes. A straightforward alternative is to introduce explicit layouts, but requiring manually specified spatial conditions reduces the flexibility of language-based interaction and does not directly resolve the misalignment between egocentric directional language and panoramic image space. To address this issue, we propose PanoCtrl, an object-centric framework for controllable text-to-panorama generation. Our method explicitly bridges natural language and spherical panoramic space by converting textual descriptions into structured object-level spherical conditions and integrating them into the diffusion process. Specifically, we introduce PanoParse, a text-conditioned parser that predicts object semantics and spherical bounding field-of-view (BFoV) parameters, and \textbf{PanoControl}, which injects object-level semantic and spatial guidance into the diffusion transformer through object-aware attention and spatial residual enhancement. To support this task, we construct PanoGround, a dataset with object-level spherical annotations and diverse directional descriptions for controllable panoramic generation. Extensive experiments demonstrate that PanoCtrl achieves state-of-the-art performance in both spatial alignment and image quality.

</details>


### [20] [ArtiMo: Agent-Driven Articulated Mesh Animation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20699)
*Chunyu Zou,Peng Dai,Yi-Hua Huang,Ze Yuan,Jingwei Huang,Yeming Yao,Xiaojuan Qi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ArtiMo is a novel zero-shot agent-driven framework for text-guided articulated 3D mesh animation. It uses LLMs/VLMs to orchestrate motion generation by combining explicit URDF kinematic constraints with agent reasoning, and includes a visual self-improvement mechanism for error correction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing data-driven mesh animation methods cannot be applied to text-guided articulated mesh animation due to: (1) strict kinematic constraints that must be satisfied, (2) need to model causal interactions between object parts, (3) requirement for instruction fidelity, and (4) absence of task-specific training data and explicit articulation supervision.

Method: ArtiMo operates in a zero-shot manner using an agentic pipeline powered by Large Language and Vision-Language Models (LLMs/VLMs). The framework: (1) synergizes explicit kinematic constraints from URDF with the agent's reasoning and planning capabilities to produce causally coherent part motions; (2) employs a visual self-improvement mechanism where generated animations are rendered into keyframes and motion cues, enabling the VLM to iteratively diagnose and correct errors.

Result: ArtiMo significantly outperforms baselines, particularly on complex, causally-driven motions. The framework was evaluated on a new benchmark dataset spanning 21 articulated object categories with high-quality motion annotations enriched with causal relationships.

Conclusion: This work presents ArtiMo, a novel agent-driven framework that successfully generates text-guided articulated mesh animations in a zero-shot setting by combining kinematic constraints with LLM/VLM reasoning and visual self-improvement. The introduced benchmark dataset advances research on causally-driven articulated motion generation.

Abstract: Animating articulated 3D meshes via text requires satisfying strict kinematic constraints, modeling causal interactions between parts, and achieving instruction fidelity. Due to the absence of task-specific training data and explicit articulation supervision, existing data-driven mesh animation methods are largely inapplicable to this setting. To address this, we propose ArtiMo, a novel agent-driven framework for text-guided articulated mesh animation. Operating in a zero-shot manner, ArtiMo develops an agentic pipeline powered by Large Language and Vision-Language Models (LLMs/VLMs) to orchestrate motion generation. By synergizing the explicit kinematic constraints of URDF with the agent's reasoning and planning capabilities, it effectively produces causally coherent part motions and interactions without requiring model fine-tuning. To ensure motion correctness, the agent additionally utilizes a visual self-improvement mechanism: generated animations are rendered into compact keyframes and motion cues, enabling the VLM to iteratively diagnose and correct errors. Furthermore, we contribute a new benchmark dataset spanning 21 articulated object categories, featuring high-quality motion annotations enriched with causal relationships. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ArtiMo significantly outperforms baselines, particularly on complex, causally driven motions. The project page is available at https://zou-2004.github.io/ArtiMo/.

</details>


### [21] [AGIDefect-4K: A Richly Annotated Dataset for AI-Generated Image Defect Detection, Localization and Explanation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20713)
*Xiangfei Sheng,Weidong Zou,Tianjiao Gu,Zhichao Yang,Pengfei Chen,Leida Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: Generative AI can now produce highly realistic images, yet current models still exhibit subtle but critical defects that undermine their reliability. While existing AI-generated image (AGI) evaluation benchmarks have made notable progress, comprehensive AGI defect diagnosis remains underexplored. To bridge this gap, we introduce AGIDefect-4K, a richly annotated dataset of 4,000 images from 15 state-of-the-art generative models spanning both open-source and closed-source systems. AGIDefect-4K features hierarchical defect annotations: (1) detection labels identifying whether defects exist, (2) pixel-level segmentation masks localizing defective regions, and (3) detailed textual explanations characterizing defect types and their perceptual impact. Each image is further annotated with an overall quality score. Building on this, we present AGIDA (AGI Defect Assistant), a baseline framework leveraging Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) for joint defect detection, localization, explanation, and quality prediction. Comprehensive benchmarking on AGIDefect-4K reveals that AGI defect understanding remains challenging, underscoring the value of this dataset. The dataset is publicly available at https://github.com/sxfly99/AGIDefect-4K.

</details>


### [22] [AffordAny: Open-World 3D Affordance Grounding from Monocular RGB Images via Vision-Language-Guided Geometric Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20720)
*Junqi Wu,Kaihua Tang,Xuanwen Chen,Hongzhi Li,Jianqiang Huang,Xian-Sheng Hua*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: Open-world 3D affordance grounding requires localizing functional object parts in 3D given free-form language queries. Existing methods typically assume pre-built object-centric 3D geometry and closed affordance ontologies, limiting deployment from raw RGB observations. We present AffordAny, an end-to-end framework that uses one monocular RGB image to construct large-scale text-conditioned 3D part supervision, ground affordances with a frozen vision-language model (VLM) guided decoder, and improve open-world generalization through pseudo-label self-training. Our automated pipeline produces a benchmark of 5,334 objects and 10,633 part-level samples spanning 473 categories, an order-of-magnitude increase in categorical diversity over prior work. The decoder progressively fuses frozen Cosmos-2B features with 3D geometry through spatial projection, instruction-conditioned semantic compression, and bidirectional geometry-semantics interaction. Minimal-perturbation pseudo-label self-training further adds new objects without human annotation. Under a systematic generalization protocol evaluating unseen objects, unseen categories, and unseen instruction paraphrases, our approach achieves 0.428 IoU on unseen objects and 0.315 IoU on unseen categories after self-training, with unseen-category mIoU improving by 6.3% relative (p<0.01) and an instruction sensitivity gap of only 0.105, demonstrating effectiveness and robustness of our method.

</details>


### [23] [VisTa3D: A Dataset and Benchmark for Thin Object Reconstruction from Vision, Tactile, and 3D Point Clouds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20740)
*Shania Guo,Yeongsik Seo,Andrew Fu,Mei Hao,Iris Xia,Jiwon Jenny Lee,Xinyi Mary Xie,Hyoungseob Park,Aaron Dollar,Alex Wong*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VisTa3D is the first thin object dataset with synchronized RGB images, depth maps, and tactile response maps, along with inertial measurements, camera pose, calibration, and groundtruth from laser scanning. It contains 387 scenes covering 70 thin objects over 17 environments. A baseline visual-range-tactile 3D reconstruction model is also introduced.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: State-of-the-art 3D reconstruction models underperform on thin objects because they occupy minimal space in RGB images and 3D point clouds, resulting in low fidelity reconstructions.

Method: Collected the first thin object dataset (VisTa3D) with synchronized RGB images, depth maps, tactile response maps, inertial measurements, camera pose/calibration, and laser-scanned groundtruth depth and segmentation maps. Benchmarked current 3D reconstruction models and introduced a baseline visual-range-tactile 3D reconstruction model to test if tactile data can aid reconstruction.

Result: Current 3D reconstruction models exhibit low fidelity on thin objects. The baseline visual-range-tactile model demonstrates that tactile data can help improve reconstruction of thin objects by providing local shape and deformation information.

Conclusion: Tactile data can aid in the reconstruction of thin objects by providing complementary local shape and deformation information that visual and range data alone cannot capture. The VisTa3D dataset enables further research in this direction.

Abstract: State-of-the-art 3D reconstruction models, whether from visual, range, or both, tend to underperform on thin objects. This is partially due to the small amount of space such objects occupy in RGB images and in 3D point clouds. To test the extent of their errors, we collected the first thin object dataset comprising of synchronized RGB images, depth maps, and tactile response maps, where each frame is associated with inertial measurements, camera pose and calibration, and groundtruth depth and segmentation maps obtained from laser scanning of thin objects. We hypothesize that tactile data can aid in the reconstruction of thin objects as their response maps provide local shape and deformation information. Our dataset, termed VisTa3D, comprises of 387 scenes covering 70 thin objects over 17 environments. We benchmarked current 3D reconstruction models on VisTa3D and found that, indeed, they exhibit low fidelity on thin objects. To test if tactile data can help, we introduce the first visual-range-tactile 3D reconstruction model as a baseline. Code and data: https://huggingface.co/datasets/shaniaguo/VisTa3D.

</details>


### [24] [Generating Multi-view Adversarial Examples for Visual Geometry Grounded Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20748)
*Qi Song,Ziyuan Luo,Haoliang Han,Renjie Wan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes MVAP-G, a multi-view adversarial perturbation generator that attacks VGGT (Visual Geometry Grounded Transformer) for 3D reconstruction by generating consistent perturbations across multiple views in a single forward pass, without requiring per-scene optimization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-performance 3D reconstruction models like VGGT have security vulnerabilities. Traditional adversarial attacks require costly per-scene optimization, while Universal Adversarial Perturbations (UAPs) fail effectively against VGGT due to its multi-view nature.

Method: MVAP-G uses a cross-view adversarial alignment mechanism to process multi-view images, ensuring perturbation consistency across diverse scenes. It generates imperceptible consistent perturbations across multiple views in a single feed-forward pass.

Result: MVAP-G significantly degrades VGGT performance without iterative optimization during inference.

Conclusion: This work pioneers multi-view adversarial attacks on 3D foundation models, revealing severe vulnerabilities and highlighting the urgent need for robust 3D vision systems.

Abstract: The Visual Geometry Grounded Transformer (VGGT) enables unified feed-forward 3D reconstruction from multi-view images. However, deploying such a high-performance model may expose critical security vulnerabilities. Traditional adversarial perturbations require costly per-scene optimization, while Universal Adversarial Perturbations (UAPs) rely on a single static pattern and fail to effectively attack VGGT. To address these limitations, we propose \textbf{MVAP-G}, a multi-view adversarial perturbation generator that produces imperceptible consistent perturbations across multiple views in a single feed-forward pass. To ensure perturbation consistency across diverse scenes, we design a cross-view adversarial alignment mechanism to process multi-view images. Experiments demonstrate that MVAP-G significantly degrades VGGT performance without iterative optimization during inference. This work pioneers multi-view adversarial attacks on 3D foundation models, uncovering severe vulnerabilities and underscoring the urgent need for robust 3D vision systems. The code is available at https://github.com/qsong2001/mvap-g.

</details>


### [25] [Identity-Preserving Text-to-Video Generation via Agentic Enhancement and Semantic Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20749)
*Jiayi Gao,Changcheng Hua,Jiaqi Tang,Yuxin Peng,Yang Liu*

Main category: cs.CV
